I know other people might say I’m fine or healthy or whatever, but it still really gets in my head. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, or do you know if your weight is on the lower side compared to what’s considered normal for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t weigh myself a lot, so I don’t want to say the wrong thing. I don’t make myself sick or anything like that. And laxatives—no. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict what or how much you eat intentionally to keep from gaining weight, like skipping meals or avoiding certain foods?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I do keep it pretty limited, but I don’t think of it as some big plan. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep things simple with eating, do you feel like it's mostly about staying light or being ready for work, or is there another reason that feels important to you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the staying light thing… like, I tell myself it’s for work, because I do need to be able to move around ladders and tight rooms and not feel sluggish.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, when I eat something heavy—pizza or a big sandwich or whatever—I get this panicky feeling that I’ll wake up bigger, or slower, and then I won’t be able to do my job the way I need to. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep things simple with eating, do you feel like it's mostly about staying light or being ready for work, or is there another reason that feels important to you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the staying light thing… like, I tell myself it’s for work, because I do need to be able to move around ladders and tight rooms and not feel sluggish.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, when I eat something heavy—pizza or a big sandwich or whatever—I get this panicky feeling that I’ll wake up bigger, or slower, and then I won’t be able to do my job the way I need to. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic. I’m not trying to make it a huge deal. That’s just kind of what goes through my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you see yourself as overweight or notice that you're afraid of gaining weight even when others might say you're at a normal or healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I see myself as overweight exactly… that sounds kind of harsh. But I do get scared of gaining, yeah.\n\nLike if I eat something heavier—pizza, or a big sandwich—I start thinking I’m going to wake up bigger, or feel slower, and then I won’t be able to move around ladders and rooms the way I need to. I know other people might say I’m fine or healthy or whatever, but it still really gets in my head. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, or do you know if your weight is on the lower side compared to what’s considered normal for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t weigh myself a lot, so I don’t want to say the wrong thing. But I have noticed my work pants are hanging pretty loose lately.\n\nAnd people on job sites have made comments, like that I look too thin, which is kind of embarrassing. I usually just think of it as I’ve leaned out because I’m on my feet all day and working, not necessarily anything bad. But… yeah, I guess other people have noticed.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your weight is significantly lower than normal for your height and age, or if you're currently underweight based on what’s considered healthy?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know the exact number, so I don’t want to say I’m officially underweight or anything if that’s not right.\n\nBut my work pants do hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin. I usually take it as I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day, you know? I don’t see it as… dangerous, exactly. But I guess other people are noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you know if your current weight is considered significantly low for someone your height and age, like in a medical sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t know in a medical sense, honestly. I haven’t really checked it that way.\n\nI just know my work pants hang pretty loose now, and people at job sites keep saying I look too thin… but I kind of figure I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. I mean, that could be right, couldn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a medical professional that your weight is considered significantly low or underweight for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. I haven’t had a doctor sit me down and say it like that.\n\nIt’s more… my work pants are a lot looser than they used to be, and guys on job sites have said I look too thin. I don’t know if that counts medically, though. I’ve been telling myself I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent blood work or medical evaluations that showed your weight is low enough to be a health concern for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had blood work or anything like that recently that said it was a health concern.\n\nIt’s more what I mentioned… my work pants are hanging pretty loose, and people on job sites will say I look too thin. I guess I’ve just been thinking I’ve leaned out from being on my feet all day. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever restrict what or how much you eat intentionally to keep from gaining weight, like skipping meals or avoiding certain foods?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess I do keep it pretty limited, but I don’t think of it as some big plan. Most mornings it’s just coffee, and then if I’m working I’ll maybe have a few bites of something between jobs. Dinner is usually small, especially if I’m busy with class paperwork or I’m tired.\n\nSo… yeah, I suppose I do skip meals sometimes, but it feels more like keeping things simple than anything extreme. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep things simple with eating, do you feel like it's mostly about staying light or being ready for work, or is there another reason that feels important to you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the staying light thing… like, I tell myself it’s for work, because I do need to be able to move around ladders and tight rooms and not feel sluggish.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, when I eat something heavy—pizza or a big sandwich or whatever—I get this panicky feeling that I’ll wake up bigger, or slower, and then I won’t be able to do my job the way I need to. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic. I’m not trying to make it a huge deal. That’s just kind of what goes through my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you see yourself as overweight or notice that you're afraid of gaining weight even when others might say you're at a normal or healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I see myself as overweight exactly… that sounds kind of harsh. But I do get scared of gaining, yeah.\n\nLike if I eat something heavier—pizza, or a big sandwich—I start thinking I’m going to wake up bigger, or feel slower, and then I won’t be able to move around ladders and rooms the way I need to. I know other people might say I’m fine or healthy or whatever, but it still really gets in my head. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, or do you know if your weight is on the lower side compared to what’s considered normal for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t weigh myself a lot, so I don’t want to say the wrong thing. But I have noticed my work pants are hanging pretty loose lately.\n\nAnd people on job sites have made comments, like that I look too thin, which is kind of embarrassing. I usually just think of it as I’ve leaned out because I’m on my feet all day and working, not necessarily anything bad. But… yeah, I guess other people have noticed.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your weight is significantly lower than normal for your height and age, or if you're currently underweight based on what’s considered healthy?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know the exact number, so I don’t want to say I’m officially underweight or anything if that’s not right.\n\nBut my work pants do hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin. I usually take it as I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day, you know? I don’t see it as… dangerous, exactly. But I guess other people are noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you know if your current weight is considered significantly low for someone your height and age, like in a medical sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t know in a medical sense, honestly. I haven’t really checked it that way.\n\nI just know my work pants hang pretty loose now, and people at job sites keep saying I look too thin… but I kind of figure I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. I mean, that could be right, couldn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a medical professional that your weight is considered significantly low or underweight for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. I haven’t had a doctor sit me down and say it like that.\n\nIt’s more… my work pants are a lot looser than they used to be, and guys on job sites have said I look too thin. I don’t know if that counts medically, though. I’ve been telling myself I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent blood work or medical evaluations that showed your weight is low enough to be a health concern for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had blood work or anything like that recently that said it was a health concern.\n\nIt’s more what I mentioned… my work pants are hanging pretty loose, and people on job sites will say I look too thin. I guess I’ve just been thinking I’ve leaned out from being on my feet all day. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, you know?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your current weight is significantly low for how tall you are, even if you haven’t had it confirmed by a doctor?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little low, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I haven’t had anyone medical tell me that.\n\nBut my work pants do hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin. I guess I’ve been thinking of it more like I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. That could be part of it, right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight is noticeably lower than what would be expected for someone your height and build, even if you haven’t had a doctor confirm it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess noticeably lower, probably. I mean, my work pants are definitely hanging looser than they used to, and people at job sites have made comments that I look too thin.\n\nI don’t know if it’s “expected for my height and build,” exactly. I’ve been thinking maybe I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day, but… yeah, I can see why it might look that way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I guess I’ve just been thinking I’ve leaned out from being on my feet all day. Dinner is usually small, especially if I’m busy with class paperwork or I’m tired.\n\nSo… yeah, I suppose I do skip meals sometimes, but it feels more like keeping things simple than anything extreme. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: When you say you keep things simple with eating, do you feel like it's mostly about staying light or being ready for work, or is there another reason that feels important to you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly the staying light thing… like, I tell myself it’s for work, because I do need to be able to move around ladders and tight rooms and not feel sluggish.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, when I eat something heavy—pizza or a big sandwich or whatever—I get this panicky feeling that I’ll wake up bigger, or slower, and then I won’t be able to do my job the way I need to. I know that probably sounds a little dramatic. I’m not trying to make it a huge deal. That’s just kind of what goes through my head.\n\nDoctor: Do you see yourself as overweight or notice that you're afraid of gaining weight even when others might say you're at a normal or healthy weight?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I see myself as overweight exactly… that sounds kind of harsh. But I do get scared of gaining, yeah.\n\nLike if I eat something heavier—pizza, or a big sandwich—I start thinking I’m going to wake up bigger, or feel slower, and then I won’t be able to move around ladders and rooms the way I need to. I know other people might say I’m fine or healthy or whatever, but it still really gets in my head. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently, or do you know if your weight is on the lower side compared to what’s considered normal for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t weigh myself a lot, so I don’t want to say the wrong thing. But I have noticed my work pants are hanging pretty loose lately.\n\nAnd people on job sites have made comments, like that I look too thin, which is kind of embarrassing. I usually just think of it as I’ve leaned out because I’m on my feet all day and working, not necessarily anything bad. But… yeah, I guess other people have noticed.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your weight is significantly lower than normal for your height and age, or if you're currently underweight based on what’s considered healthy?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know the exact number, so I don’t want to say I’m officially underweight or anything if that’s not right.\n\nBut my work pants do hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin. I usually take it as I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day, you know? I don’t see it as… dangerous, exactly. But I guess other people are noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you know if your current weight is considered significantly low for someone your height and age, like in a medical sense?\n\nPatient: I don’t know in a medical sense, honestly. I haven’t really checked it that way.\n\nI just know my work pants hang pretty loose now, and people at job sites keep saying I look too thin… but I kind of figure I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. I mean, that could be right, couldn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a medical professional that your weight is considered significantly low or underweight for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no. I haven’t had a doctor sit me down and say it like that.\n\nIt’s more… my work pants are a lot looser than they used to be, and guys on job sites have said I look too thin. I don’t know if that counts medically, though. I’ve been telling myself I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent blood work or medical evaluations that showed your weight is low enough to be a health concern for someone your height and age?\n\nPatient: No, I haven’t had blood work or anything like that recently that said it was a health concern.\n\nIt’s more what I mentioned… my work pants are hanging pretty loose, and people on job sites will say I look too thin. I guess I’ve just been thinking I’ve leaned out from being on my feet all day. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, you know?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your current weight is significantly low for how tall you are, even if you haven’t had it confirmed by a doctor?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little low, yeah. I don’t want to overstate it, because I haven’t had anyone medical tell me that.\n\nBut my work pants do hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin. I guess I’ve been thinking of it more like I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. That could be part of it, right?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your weight is noticeably lower than what would be expected for someone your height and build, even if you haven’t had a doctor confirm it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess noticeably lower, probably. I’ve been thinking maybe I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day, but… yeah, I can see why it might look that way.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your current weight is significantly low for your height and age based on medical standards, even if you haven’t had someone formally tell you that?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know by medical standards, honestly. I haven’t had someone tell me that officially.\n\nI mean… my work pants are pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin, so maybe it’s lower than it should be. But I’ve kind of been telling myself I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. Is that… does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your current weight is significantly low for your height and age based on medical standards, even if you haven't had someone formally tell you that?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say “by medical standards,” because no one’s formally told me that. But… yeah, my work pants do hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites keep saying I look too thin.\n\nI guess I’ve been seeing it more like I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day, so I don’t know if that counts as significantly low. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if your current weight, based on your height and age, meets the medical definition of being underweight, such as a low BMI?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about the BMI part, honestly. I haven’t really had anyone tell me, like, “this is medically underweight.”\n\nI do know my work pants hang pretty loose now, and people on job sites have said I look too thin… but I guess I’ve been thinking of it more like I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. I don’t know if that counts as a low BMI or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you know what your Body Mass Index (BMI) is, or if it falls into the underweight range according to medical standards?\n\nPatient: I don’t know my actual BMI, no. I haven’t checked it or had someone explain where it falls.\n\nI can tell my work pants are hanging pretty loose now, and people on job sites have said I look too thin… but I guess I’ve been thinking of it more like I’ve just leaned out from being on my feet all day. I’m not sure if that means it’s medically underweight.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
